It doesn’t feel like me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way—weeks, or has it been going on more continuously for a year or more?\n\nPatient: It’s been more continuous—about a year and three months now. It started after a family member’s health and behavior changed, and I ended up taking on a lot more of the coordination.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, have you had any distinct periods—lasting several days—where you felt unusually energetic, elated, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do have stretches where I feel unusually upbeat, like everything is clicking and I’m really sure of myself. But then something small—like someone moving a meeting—can set me off, and I get sharp with people in a way that surprises me.\n\nDoctor: When you have those upbeat, energized stretches, how many days in a row do they typically last?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t kept track of the number of days—I just notice I’m more up and reactive for a while, then it fades.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical changes recently, like unexplained weight gain, feeling unusually cold, dry skin, or constipation?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed any physical changes like that. Nothing stands out to me recently.\n\nDoctor: Besides the low mood and loss of interest, have you noticed changes in your sleep, appetite, concentration, or energy that occur nearly every day during this time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really paid attention to whether those things are changing every day.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a period of at least two straight weeks where this low, flat mood was so intense that it seriously interfered with your ability to work or manage daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I could say it was two straight weeks exactly, but it got to where I stopped initiating things I normally care about—my photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar organized. Everything just felt flat and heavy.\n\nDoctor: Since this began about 15 months ago, has your low, flat mood been present more days than not, without any stretches of feeling essentially back to your normal self, or have there been clear breaks where the low mood lifted completely for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t tracked it closely enough to know whether there were weeks where I felt fully back to normal.\n\nDoctor: During those upbeat, energized stretches, have people around you noticed a clear change in your behavior or mood compared to your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I can get unusually upbeat, like everything is really clicking, and then something small—someone moving a meeting, for example—can make me snap or get sharp with people. I haven’t paid enough attention to how many days they last to say whether it’s four days or just a day or two.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven’t really kept track well enough to know whether there was a full two-month stretch where I felt completely back to normal.\n\nDoctor: During your lowest periods, was there ever a stretch of at least two consecutive weeks where the flat, heavy mood caused you to actually miss work, skip important commitments, or fail to keep up with basic responsibilities every single day?\n\nPatient: Not every day, no.
